"""Validate external references, own descriptive numbers and exact PDF citation targets."""
from pathlib import Path
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
import csv,hashlib,json,re,urllib.request,urllib.parse
import fitz,numpy as np,pandas as pd
W=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1];M=W.parent/'MiniProject/Milestone 2'
O=M/'respaldo/estado_del_arte/entrega_cinco_paginas_v022'
def sha(p):return hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
def dump(p,d):p.write_text(json.dumps(d,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\n',encoding='utf-8')
d=json.loads((W/'templates/milestone2_content.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
headers={'User-Agent':'DS675 academic reference verification/1.0','Accept':'application/json,text/html'}
def get(url,json_mode=True):
 with urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request(url,headers=headers),timeout=18) as r:
  data=r.read();return (json.loads(data) if json_mode else data),r.status,r.url
def check(row):
 label,url=row;number=int(re.match(r'^\[(\d+)\]',label)[1]);item={'number':number,'label':label,'url':url}
 try:
  if 'doi.org/' in url:
   doi=url.split('doi.org/',1)[1];meta,status,target=get('https://api.crossref.org/works/'+urllib.parse.quote(doi,safe=''))
   record=meta['message'];assert record['DOI'].lower()==doi.lower()
   item.update(verified=True,method='Crossref registered DOI record',metadata_status=status,registered_title=record['title'][0],registered_authors=[r.get('family','') for r in record.get('author',[])],registry_url=target)
  elif 'zenodo.org/records/' in url:
   rid=url.rsplit('/',1)[1];meta,status,target=get('https://zenodo.org/api/records/'+rid)
   assert str(meta['id'])==rid
   item.update(verified=True,method='Original Zenodo record API',metadata_status=status,registered_title=meta['metadata']['title'],registered_authors=[r['name'] for r in meta['metadata'].get('creators',[])],registry_url=target)
  elif 'github.com/' in url:
   parts=urllib.parse.urlparse(url).path.strip('/').split('/');owner,repo=parts[:2];commit=parts[3]
   meta,status,target=get('https://api.github.com/repos/'+owner+'/'+repo+'/commits/'+commit)
   assert meta['sha']==commit
   item.update(verified=True,method='Original GitHub commit API',metadata_status=status,commit=commit,registry_url=target)
  else:
   data,status,target=get(url,False);assert status==200
   assert any(name in data.decode('utf-8',errors='replace') for name in ['Ridge','RandomForestRegressor','GradientBoostingRegressor'])
   item.update(verified=True,method='Official documentation HTTP content',status=status,resolved_url=target)
 except Exception as e:item.update(verified=False,error=type(e).__name__+': '+str(e))
 return item
cached=O/'external_reference_check.json'
previous=json.loads(cached.read_text(encoding='utf-8')) if cached.exists() else None
if previous and previous.get('all_verified') and [r['url'] for r in previous['references']]==[url for label,url in d['references']]:
 references=previous['references']
 for row,(label,url) in zip(references,d['references']):row['label']=label
else:
 with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=6) as pool:references=list(pool.map(check,d['references']))
dump(O/'external_reference_check.json',{'references':references,'self_domain_references':0,'all_verified':all(r['verified'] for r in references)})

assert all(r['verified'] for r in references),'External reference verification incomplete'

C=M/'respaldo/datos/oficial/csv_verificados';frames={};struct=[]
for year in [2018,2019]:
 for kind,n in [('Pressures',33),('Flows',3),('Levels',1),('Demands',82)]:
  path=C/f'{year}_SCADA_{kind}.csv';f=pd.read_csv(path,sep=';',decimal=',',index_col=0,parse_dates=True)
  assert f.shape==(105120,n) and f.index.is_unique and f.index.is_monotonic_increasing and not f.isna().any().any()
  assert (f.index.to_series().diff().dropna()==pd.Timedelta(minutes=5)).all()
  struct.append({'file':path.name,'shape':list(f.shape),'missing':0,'sha256':sha(path),'scope':'structure only' if year==2019 else 'structure plus Jan-Sep descriptive EDA'})
  if year==2018:frames[kind]=f.loc['2018-01-01':'2018-09-30']
p=frames['Pressures'];x=pd.DataFrame({n:p[n] for n in ['n1','n4','n31']})
x['T1']=frames['Levels'].T1;x['PUMP_1']=frames['Flows'].PUMP_1;x['AMR_n1']=frames['Demands'].n1
x['n1 +15']=p.n1.shift(-3);pairs=x.dropna();err=pairs['n1 +15']-pairs.n1
eda={'rows':len(p),'days':len(p.index.normalize().unique()),'pairs':len(pairs),'MAE':float(err.abs().mean()),'RMSE':float(np.sqrt((err**2).mean())),'range':[float(p.n1.min()),float(p.n1.max())],'correlations':{c:float(pairs[c].corr(pairs['n1 +15'])) for c in ['n1','T1','PUMP_1','AMR_n1']}}
assert eda['rows']==78624 and eda['days']==273 and eda['pairs']==78621

### aggregated_output

```text
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np


def global_preprocessing(X_data, leak_labels, time_start, time_win_len, sensors_idx=None):
    X = []
    Y = []
    y_leak = []
    
    if sensors_idx is None:
        sensors_idx = list(range(X_data.shape[1]))
    
     # Use a sliding time window to construct a labeled data set
    t_index = time_start
    time_points = range(len(leak_labels))
    i = 0
    while t_index < len(time_points) - time_win_len:
        # Grab time window from data stream
        x = X_data[t_index:t_index+time_win_len-1, sensors_idx]

        #######################
        # Feature engineering #
        #######################
        x = np.mean(x,axis=0)  # "Stupid" feature
        X.append(x)

        Y.append([X_data[t_index + time_win_len-1, n] for n in sensors_idx])

        y_leak.append(leak_labels[t_index + time_win_len-1])

        t_index += 1  # Note: Overlapping time windows
        i += 1

    X = np.array(X)
    Y = np.array(Y)
    y_leak = np.array(y_leak)

    return X, Y, y_leak
import sys
import os
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.metrics import mean_squared_error

from LeakDbScenarioPreprocessing import global_preprocessing
from MyModel import MyModel, create_model
from FaultDetection import EnsembleSystem
from utils import evaluate_fault_detection
from models import KNN, LinearModel
from SAM_KNN_Regression import SAMKNNRegressor


path_to_data = "data/"

time_win_len = 4  # Always consider the last four time steps
time_start = 100  # Ignore the first 100 samples

t_train_split = 3000    # Use the first 3000 sampels for training -- there are no anomalies present early until end of January!  
batch_size = 200  # Number of samples used for adapting the model to the fault


def load_data(scenario_id, use_faulty_sensor_scenario=False):
    # Open and read file
    df_pressures = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/Results/Measurements_Pressures.csv"))
    df_flows = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/Results/Measurements_Flows.csv"))
    
    # Parse labels
    df_labels = df_pressures[["Timestamp"]].copy()
    df_labels["label"] = 0

    def parse_leak_data(): # TODO: Assuming there is only one leaky node!
        leaky_pipe, start, end = None, None, None
        
        # Locate and load file with information on the leak
        leak_info_file = list(filter(lambda z: z.endswith(".xlsx"), os.listdir(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsLeakages/"))))[0]
        df_leak_info = pd.read_excel(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsLeakages/{leak_info_file}"), sheet_name="Info", engine='openpyxl')
        
        # Parse for start and end time of leak
        for _, row in df_leak_info.iterrows():
            if row["Description"] == "Leak Start":
                start = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Leak End":
                end = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Leak Pipe":
                leaky_pipe = row["Value"]
        
        return leaky_pipe, start, end

    leaky_pipe, start, end = parse_leak_data()

    # Load faulty sensor information
    def load_faulty_sensor_info():
        faulty_sensor_id, start, end = None, None, None

        faulty_sensor_info_file = list(filter(lambda z: z.endswith(".csv"), os.listdir(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/WithoutSensorFaults/"))))[0]
        df_faulty_sensor_info = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/WithoutSensorFaults/{faulty_sensor_info_file}"))
        for _, row in df_faulty_sensor_info.iterrows():
            if row["Description"] == "Sensor":
                faulty_sensor_id = row["Value"].replace("node", "") # Remove naming artificat from the generator
            elif row["Description"] == "Fault Start":
                start = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Fault End":
                end = row["Value"]

        return faulty_sensor_id, start, end
    
    faulty_sensor_id = None
    if use_faulty_sensor_scenario is True:
        faulty_sensor_id, start, end = load_faulty_sensor_info()    # Note: We override start and end time of the leakage

    if use_faulty_sensor_scenario is True:  # Data from which simulation should be used?
        df_pressures = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/Measurements_Pressures.csv"))
        df_flows = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/Measurements_Flows.csv"))

    # Parse labels
    indices = df_labels[(df_labels["Timestamp"] >= start) & (df_labels["Timestamp"] <= end)].index
    for idx in indices:
        df_labels["label"].loc[idx] = 1
```

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np


def global_preprocessing(X_data, leak_labels, time_start, time_win_len, sensors_idx=None):
    X = []
    Y = []
    y_leak = []
    
    if sensors_idx is None:
        sensors_idx = list(range(X_data.shape[1]))
    
     # Use a sliding time window to construct a labeled data set
    t_index = time_start
    time_points = range(len(leak_labels))
    i = 0
    while t_index < len(time_points) - time_win_len:
        # Grab time window from data stream
        x = X_data[t_index:t_index+time_win_len-1, sensors_idx]

        #######################
        # Feature engineering #
        #######################
        x = np.mean(x,axis=0)  # "Stupid" feature
        X.append(x)

        Y.append([X_data[t_index + time_win_len-1, n] for n in sensors_idx])

        y_leak.append(leak_labels[t_index + time_win_len-1])

        t_index += 1  # Note: Overlapping time windows
        i += 1

    X = np.array(X)
    Y = np.array(Y)
    y_leak = np.array(y_leak)

    return X, Y, y_leak
import sys
import os
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.metrics import mean_squared_error

from LeakDbScenarioPreprocessing import global_preprocessing
from MyModel import MyModel, create_model
from FaultDetection import EnsembleSystem
from utils import evaluate_fault_detection
from models import KNN, LinearModel
from SAM_KNN_Regression import SAMKNNRegressor


path_to_data = "data/"

time_win_len = 4  # Always consider the last four time steps
time_start = 100  # Ignore the first 100 samples

t_train_split = 3000    # Use the first 3000 sampels for training -- there are no anomalies present early until end of January!  
batch_size = 200  # Number of samples used for adapting the model to the fault


def load_data(scenario_id, use_faulty_sensor_scenario=False):
    # Open and read file
    df_pressures = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/Results/Measurements_Pressures.csv"))
    df_flows = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/Results/Measurements_Flows.csv"))
    
    # Parse labels
    df_labels = df_pressures[["Timestamp"]].copy()
    df_labels["label"] = 0

    def parse_leak_data(): # TODO: Assuming there is only one leaky node!
        leaky_pipe, start, end = None, None, None
        
        # Locate and load file with information on the leak
        leak_info_file = list(filter(lambda z: z.endswith(".xlsx"), os.listdir(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsLeakages/"))))[0]
        df_leak_info = pd.read_excel(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsLeakages/{leak_info_file}"), sheet_name="Info", engine='openpyxl')
        
        # Parse for start and end time of leak
        for _, row in df_leak_info.iterrows():
            if row["Description"] == "Leak Start":
                start = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Leak End":
                end = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Leak Pipe":
                leaky_pipe = row["Value"]
        
        return leaky_pipe, start, end

    leaky_pipe, start, end = parse_leak_data()

    # Load faulty sensor information
    def load_faulty_sensor_info():
        faulty_sensor_id, start, end = None, None, None

        faulty_sensor_info_file = list(filter(lambda z: z.endswith(".csv"), os.listdir(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/WithoutSensorFaults/"))))[0]
        df_faulty_sensor_info = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/WithoutSensorFaults/{faulty_sensor_info_file}"))
        for _, row in df_faulty_sensor_info.iterrows():
            if row["Description"] == "Sensor":
                faulty_sensor_id = row["Value"].replace("node", "") # Remove naming artificat from the generator
            elif row["Description"] == "Fault Start":
                start = row["Value"]
            elif row["Description"] == "Fault End":
                end = row["Value"]

        return faulty_sensor_id, start, end
    
    faulty_sensor_id = None
    if use_faulty_sensor_scenario is True:
        faulty_sensor_id, start, end = load_faulty_sensor_info()    # Note: We override start and end time of the leakage

    if use_faulty_sensor_scenario is True:  # Data from which simulation should be used?
        df_pressures = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/Measurements_Pressures.csv"))
        df_flows = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(path_to_data, f"Scenario-{scenario_id}/ResultsSensorFaults/Measurements_Flows.csv"))

    # Parse labels
    indices = df_labels[(df_labels["Timestamp"] >= start) & (df_labels["Timestamp"] <= end)].index
    for idx in indices:
        df_labels["label"].loc[idx] = 1

    labels = df_labels["label"].to_numpy().flatten()

    # Parse pressure and flow measurements
    df_pressures.drop(columns=["Timestamp"], inplace=True)
    df_flows.drop(columns=["Timestamp"], inplace=True)

    pressure_nodes = list(df_pressures.columns)
    flow_nodes = list(df_flows.columns)
    nodes = pressure_nodes + flow_nodes

    pressures_per_node = {}
    for node_id in pressure_nodes:
        pressures_per_node[node_id] = df_pressures[[node_id]].to_numpy().flatten()

    flows_per_node = {}
    for node_id in flow_nodes:
        flows_per_node[node_id] = df_flows[[node_id]].to_numpy().flatten()

    # Build numpy arrays
    y = labels
    X = np.vstack([pressures_per_node[n] for n in pressure_nodes] + [flows_per_node[n] for n in flow_nodes]).T

    return X, y, nodes, pressure_nodes, flow_nodes, leaky_pipe, faulty_sensor_id


def score(Y_True, Y_pred):
    Y_pred = Y_pred.reshape(Y_True.shape)
    return [mean_squared_error(Y_True[:, i], Y_pred[:, i]) for i in range(Y_True.shape[1])]


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Parse arguments
    if len(sys.argv) != 4:
        print("Usage: <scenario-id> <model-desc> <use_faulty_sensor_scenario>")
        os._exit(1)
    
    scenario_id = sys.argv[1]
    model_desc = sys.argv[2]
    use_faulty_sensor_scenario = True if sys.argv[3] == "True" else False

    # Load data
    X, y, nodes, pressure_nodes, flow_nodes, leaky_pipe, faulty_sensor = load_data(scenario_id, use_faulty_sensor_scenario)

    # Preprocessing
    X_all, Y_all, leak_labels_all = global_preprocessing(X, y, time_start, time_win_len, list(range(len(pressure_nodes))))  
    n_dim = X_all.shape[1]

    X_all_train, X_all_test = X_all[:t_train_split,:], X_all[t_train_split:,:]
    Y_all_train, Y_all_test = Y_all[:t_train_split,:], Y_all[t_train_split:,:]
    fault_labels_all_train, fault_labels_all_test = leak_labels_all[:t_train_split], leak_labels_all[t_train_split:]

    faulty_times = np.where(fault_labels_all_test == 1)[0]
    test_t0, test_t1= faulty_times[0], faulty_times[-1] + 50  # Time period in the test set where a fault is present + some a time buffer for the original hydraulics to recover from the fault

    # Fit model
    def get_model():
        if model_desc == "knn":
            return KNN(n_dim-1, k=5, window_size=500)
        elif model_desc == "linear":
            return LinearModel(n_dim-1)
        elif model_desc == "samknn":
            return SAMKNNRegressor(multi_dim_y=False, max_LTM_size=100, n_neighbors=5, error_type="MAE")

    my_model_gen = lambda: create_model(get_model())
    model = EnsembleSystem(my_model_gen, flow_nodes, pressure_nodes)
    #model = EnsembleSystem(MyModel, flow_nodes, pressure_nodes)
    model.fit(X_all_train, Y_all_train)

    # Online learning -- test if anomalies are found
    suspicous_time_points = []
    t = 0
    while t + batch_size < X_all_test.shape[0]:
        X_batch, Y_batch = X_all_test[t:t+batch_size,:], Y_all_test[t:t+batch_size,:]

        # Apply anomaly detection to current batch
        time_points, _ = model.apply_detector(X_batch, Y_batch)
        for tp in time_points:
            suspicous_time_points.append(t + tp)

```

Graph filters, adaptive memories and CUSUM extend the class baselines; their source files show the mechanisms [4,6,9].\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    [\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"h\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"3.1 Anticipated contribution relative to prior work\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"We propose Gradient Boosting (GradientBoostingRegressor) for recorded n1(+15) using the six local signals. It offers a course-level alternative to the graph/recurrent and adaptive-kNN predictors above. Boosting already classifies L-Town leakage regions [13,14]; Artesia estimates night pressure with ExtraTrees but specifies no future lead [15], pp. 1-2. Our review did not identify this exact six-signal n1(+15) experiment. The expected contribution is a reproducible comparison on the released SCADA, including operating/leak conditions. It would test a useful extension of the reviewed solutions; it is not a new algorithm or a claim of worldwide priority.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"table\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          [\\r\\n            [\\r\\n              \\\"Planned output\\\",\\r\\n              \\\"What we expect to learn\\\"\\r\\n            ],\\r\\n            [\\r\\n              \\\"C1. Forecast-ready table\\\",\\r\\n              \\\"Which past inputs belong to each +15 label; reproducible time-based splits.\\\"\\r\\n            ],\\r\\n            [\\r\\n              \\\"C2. Boosted-tree forecast\\\",\\r\\n              \\\"Whether boosting reduces error relative to persistence, Ridge and Random Forest.\\\"\\r\\n            ],\\r\\n            [\\r\\n              \\\"C3. These give 72 columns. Add sine/cosine of clock time and seven weekday indicators: 81 inputs, or 21 inputs for n1-only. These are derived columns, not extra sensors. Align timestamps/check units and missing values; set y to n1 three timestamps later. Remove incomplete rows and labels belonging to the next split. Fit Ridge scaling using training data only; leak labels stay outside X. Section 1 EDA motivates the baselines but does not prove that all six signals help.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"math\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"\\\\\\\\widehat p_{n1}(u+15)=F_M(X(u)),\\\\\\\\qquad F_m(X)=F_{m-1}(X)+\\\\\\\\eta h_m(X).\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Boosting starts with the training-target mean and adds shallow trees that learn the remaining prediction errors; eta controls each correction [18]. The target remains absolute future pressure. This is the sequential error-correction idea of Module 11.6/Week 7. Ridge is a linear model with a penalty on large coefficients [16]. Random Forest averages trees fitted to resampled training rows [17]. They provide a linear and a tree-ensemble reference for the same task.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Initial model settings, to be checked on validation data: boosting stages {100,200,300} × learning rates {0.03,0.1}, depth=2, minimum leaf=20, squared-error loss, subsample=1; no random-split early stopping. Forest: 200 trees, max_features=1.0, depth {6,10,14} × minimum leaf {10,40}. Ridge alpha: {0.01,0.1,1,10,100,1000}. Use tree seed 42 and six settings per learner/input set, chosen by validation MAE [16,17,18]. These are proposed choices, not proven best settings.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"h\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"3.3 Evaluation and criteria for improvement\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Train January-September 2018; choose settings October-December 2018; then refit on eligible 2018 and freeze settings before the 2019 assessment. Keep time order and the same forecast rows for every model, with 55 min of past readings. Remove labels that cross a split boundary; past inputs may come from the previous period. Since 2019 was already inspected, it is a final time-based assessment, not a blind test. Compare persistence, Ridge, Forest, full boosting and n1-only boosting. In validation, remove T1, PUMP_1, n4/n31 or AMR_n1 one group at a time, following the class attribute-removal idea.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Recompute MAE/RMSE on these common 2019 rows; report errors in metres and skill=1-MAE_model/MAE_persistence (positive means better than persistence). A boosting advantage requires lower MAE than persistence, Ridge and Forest; auxiliary value requires beating n1-only boosting and persistence. Report monthly errors, RMSE tradeoffs and unsuccessful results; these comparisons do not prove statistical significance. Deliver the preparation/model notebooks, settings, score table and prediction/error plots. No learning model has been fitted. Delays/ages, balance proxies and block intervals remain outside this scope.\\\"\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    [\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"kind\\\": \\\"p\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": [\\r\\n          \\\"Numbered citations link to the entries below; entries open the original article, author repository or official documentation. 445-6    Summary and Conclusion
446-Inspired by the SAM-kNN classiﬁer, we proposed SAM-kNN regressor as an
447-online learner for regression. In contrast to other online learners, our proposed
448-method comes with a memory component which allows it to remember past
449-concepts quite easily. We empirically evaluated our proposed online learner in
450:an anomaly detection scenario for a realistic water distribution network – our
451-proposed online learner consistently outperforms other standard online learners.
452-    Although our proposed method shows good performance for leakage and
453-sensor fault detection (two very common anomalies), it is unclear, how well it
454-performs for other (more complex) types of anomalies such as cyber-physical
455-attacks, etc. Furthermore, another challenge is high-dimensional data – in this
456:work our data had 29 dimensions which is already somewhat high but can still be
457-managed by our kNN based method. However, in case of really high dimensional
458-data, kNN will encounter performance problems – e.g. some kind of integrated
459-dimensionality reduction might be required. We leave these aspects as future
460-work.

# SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning in Water Distribution Networks

This repository contains the implementation of the methods proposed in the paper [SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning in Water Distribution Networks](paper.pdf) by Jonathan Jakob, AndrÃ© Artelt, Martina HasenjÃ¤ger and Barbara Hammer.

The experiments as described in the paper are implemented in the folder [Implementation](Implementation/).

## Abstract
Water distribution networks are a key component of modern infrastructure for housing and industry. They transport and distribute water via widely branched networks from sources to consumers.
In order to guarantee a working network at all times, the water supply company continuously monitors the network and takes actions when necessary -- e.g. reacting to leakages, sensor faults and drops in water quality. Since real world networks are too large and complex to be monitored by a human, algorithmic monitoring systems have been developed. A popular type of such systems are residual based anomaly detection systems that can detect events such as leakages and sensor faults. For a continuous high quality monitoring, it is necessary for these systems to adapt to changed demands and presence of various anomalies.

In this work, we propose an adaption of the incremental SAM-kNN classifier for regression to build a residual based anomaly detection system for water distribution networks that is able to adapt to any kind of change.

## Details
### Implementaton of experiments
The shell script `run_experiments.sh` runs all experiments 

### Other (important) stuff
#### `SAM_KNN_Regression.py`
Implementation of our proposed SAM-kNN regressor.

## Data

Note that we did not publish the data sets due to large file sizes. Please contact us if you are interested in the data sets.

## Requirements

- Python3.6
- Packages as listed in `Implementation/REQUIREMENTS.txt`

## License

MIT license - See [LICENSE](LICENSE)

Yan, J., Tian, C., Wang, Y., Huang, J.: Online incremental regression for electricity\r\n# SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning in Water Distribution Networks\r\n\r\nThis repository contains the implementation of the methods proposed in the paper [SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning in Water Distribution Networks](paper.pdf) by Jonathan Jakob, AndrÃ© Artelt, Martina HasenjÃ¤ger and Barbara Hammer.\r\n\r\nThe experiments as described in the paper are implemented in the folder [Implementation](Implementation/).\r\n\r\n## Abstract\r\nWater distribution networks are a key component of modern infrastructure for housing and industry. They transport and distribute water via widely branched networks from sources to consumers.\r\nIn order to guarantee a working network at all times, the water supply company continuously monitors the network and takes actions when necessary -- e.g. reacting to leakages, sensor faults and drops in water quality. Since real world networks are too large and complex to be monitored by a human, algorithmic monitoring systems have been developed. A popular type of such systems are residual based anomaly detection systems that can detect events such as leakages and sensor faults. For a continuous high quality monitoring, it is necessary for these systems to adapt to changed demands and presence of various anomalies.\r\n\r\nIn this work, we propose an adaption of the incremental SAM-kNN classifier for regression to build a residual based anomaly detection system for water distribution networks that is able to adapt to any kind of change.\r\n\r\n## Details\r\n### Implementaton of experiments\r\nThe shell script `run_experiments.sh` runs all experiments \r\n\r\n### Other (important) stuff\r\n#### `SAM_KNN_Regression.py`\r\nImplementation of our proposed SAM-kNN regressor.\r\n\r\n## Data\r\n\r\nNote that we did not publish the data sets due to large file sizes. Please contact us if you are interested in the data sets.\r\n\r\n## Requirements\r\n\r\n- Python3.6\r\n- Packages as listed in `Implementation/REQUIREMENTS.txt`\r\n\r\n## License\r\n\r\nMIT license - See [LICENSE](LICENSE)\r\n"
